Translator's Introduction

When I started translating this book, I had no knowledge of its contents and I did not know the author.
I was not familiar with the book. But when I went a little further, I realized how amazingly interesting this book was.
Motivation is useful for both Muslims and Christians. The word of God in (Hosea 4:6)
He says, "...my people have perished for lack of knowledge." When he goes from one part of the book to another,
I was beginning to realize how much my previous knowledge about Islam and Muslims was wrong.
It was weak and insignificant. As a Christian with an Islamic background, I found that most of the information
My views on Islam are based on false and superstitious ideas and have no connection to the truth.
I truly believe that this book will serve as a valuable resource for you, as it relates to
Have an effective relationship with your Muslim friends, neighbors, and colleagues, and ultimately, success.
You will have more success in leading them to Christ and salvation. I recommend that you read this book when you
You are invited to go to God in prayer and ask Him to help you understand the contents and
Use the truths in this book to save people and bring them to the foot of the cross of Christ.

• Biblical texts are quoted from the Old Testament and the Commentary, and are otherwise identified.
Biblical references are abbreviated and can be found in the appendix.
• Quotations from the Quran are listed by verse number and are adapted from the Persian translation by Fawad Vand.
The verses in different translations of the Quran are slightly different.
• Arabic names and words are given with the same common English pronunciation. These words are either in their original place
The word is explained or explained in the appendix section (4.4 Arabic/Islamic Vocabulary).
Arabic words that have not yet been processed into English (for further explanation, see the Standard Dictionary)
See) is in italics.
• For greater convenience, feminine forms such as "Maslameh" have been ignored and the general form of the word has been used instead.
Muslim (Muslim) is used.
• References are abbreviated in the footnotes. Information about the history of the books is in the appendix section.
(4.1 Bibliography and suggested reading) is provided.
• Since there is a different understanding of the concept of God in the Quran and the Bible, I use the term "God" in Islamic texts.
I used it.

Introduction

After World War II, Muslim nations gradually began a movement to elevate themselves to become
They started a powerful independent religious and political state. Due to unprecedented immigration, in most non-Muslim countries,
Muslims became neighbors with them. Recent events such as September 11, 2001 in the United States and numerous terrorist attacks in other countries.
It put Muslim regions at the center of public attention, and naturally questions arose in minds about this religion.
It is now clear that Islam is not a religion that can be accepted by the public as a personal religious belief. In contrast,
Islam claims to be a religion that transcends every individual and nation in every aspect of life, such as faith, morality,
Law, social order, art, literature, economics, and politics. In this book I have tried to present a perspective on religion.
Islam and its vicious attacks against the original biblical faith. I will examine how Christians
They can relate confidently and lovingly to their Muslim neighbors and try to understand their often strange perceptions.
I have tried to organize the text in a systematic manner for easy understanding. For a more comprehensive study of the subject, please refer to
We suggest readers visit our website or read the books suggested in Section 4.1 of the Appendices.
Four emphatic points
1. This book is written in a structured manner to provide a basic insight into the religion of Islam and how it works.
present the religious beliefs of Muslims. After reading this book, readers will understand that in many
They know more about Islam than the average Muslim. But the point is not that Christians
They should be able to correct the mistakes of Muslims about their faith. On the contrary, Christians should have a great interest in
Show them what Muslims say.
8. Information on this can be compared with statements made by Muslims and help Christians.
To help them understand the difference between reality and the delusions of Muslims.
1. Accordingly, Christians can then ask very effective questions to challenge Muslims.
Ask them to think about the relationship between themselves and their Creator. Instead of immediately answering
Present questions. It is better to challenge Muslims to think and answer the question.
They will find it themselves.
4. After these steps, Christians can share their faith with Muslims and the teachings of the Bible.
Explain the sacred to them.

Muslim: I must pray five times a day to be a good Muslim!
Christian: Oh, how interesting! Can you please show me where it says you have to pray five times a day?
Muslim: It's written in the Quran!
Christian: Well, I'd like to read it myself - can you give me the address of the verse?
Muslim: I have to find him first.
...after a while:
Muslim: These are texts in the Quran regarding prayer (...Note: Nowhere in the Quran is it explicitly stated that Muslims
They should pray five times a day. Christians should make Muslims aware of such realities in a loving way without
Make fun of them)
Christian: Thank you for showing me these Quranic texts. I can also show you what the Bible says about the issue of
What does prayer say?

Conversation 2

“Christian: Tell me how you became a Muslim.
Muslim: Well, I was born in Saudi Arabia!
Christian: If you were born in Japan, would you become a Buddhist? This often leads to a lot of discussion, and perhaps this
The question leads to:
Muslim: So how did you become a Christian?

Their wrong behaviors will disappear.

Asking them intelligent questions requires a basic understanding of Islam and the Bible.

Notes
1. The purpose of asking Muslims questions is not to embarrass or shame them (perhaps
They don't know the answer) or we shouldn't provoke them into an aggressive reaction.
Christians must adopt an approach consistent with Christian love that leads them to change.
Encourage a deeper perspective and a serious response (see sections 1.1 and 1.8).
This is a unique and different situation; therefore, Christians must be careful in choosing the questions they will answer in this
Suggested books, have wisdom. Not every question is appropriate for every situation. Often
It's reasonable to ask a general question, what do you think about this? If the conditions were right,
We will ask specific questions.
8. Apart from asking questions, there are other valuable ways to approach Muslims.
Giving the gospel urgently is one possible way, but in all cases being sensitive to
The guidance of the Holy Spirit is decisive.

Christians are committed to having accurate information about Islam. Popular perceptions are often based on inaccurate information.
It is misguided, and instead of providing help, it fuels fear.

7.7 History of Islam
The Arabian Peninsula was the birthplace of Islam. 2. It was here that Muhammad, the Prophet of Islam, lived and died. The city
The cities of Mecca and Medina play a special and important role in the history of Islam. Before the advent of Islam, Arabia was not very
It was not known because all the relevant factors had been destroyed by the advent of Islam. Many things
That reported in news and hadiths should be carefully examined and analyzed for accuracy and reliability.


Why is studying history important? ...Is this a true story?
When Muhammad fled Mecca, he hid from his enemies in a cave. A spider was in front of the small entrance.
The cave spun a web. And when Muhammad's pursuers came, they saw the spider's web in front of the cave and took it with them.
They said: "It is impossible that he has hidden here." So they left there and continued on their way. (Muslims)
They say) Isn't it amazing how Allah took care of His Prophet?


7.7.7 Arabia before Muhammad.
Tribal life in Arabia: Muslims call the period before Muhammad (500 AD) the Age of Ignorance.
The great world powers in the 6th century AD controlled the eastern countries: the Byzantine Empire and the
Sassanid 6. Arabia was a somewhat free and independent world, alongside the two great power spheres of that period.
The Arabian Peninsula bordered both territories and was therefore somewhat affected and involved in the conflict.
1
Because of these increasing conflicts, both empires were weakened and thus became empty.
A power was emerging. Trade flourished along the caravan route along the Red Sea, and
The Silk Road connected East Africa with East Asia and Europe. The city of Mecca became an important center.
It had become a commercial center, but it was also a religious center and a pilgrimage city. The sale of idols was a source of
The most important thing in this city was income. Social inequality and injustice were rampant. Having many daughters
It was considered a disgrace for a father. Therefore, these girls were buried alive immediately after birth. This
It was an example of the little rights and protection that societies gave to women.




1. Many Muslims disagree and consider Adam to be the first Muslim.
1. Muslims give many reasons for this, the main one being that the Arabs worshipped many idols.
They worshipped, not knowing that there was only one God.
4. The Sassanid Empire or Sassanid Persian dynasty lasted from 882 to 251 AD. Their main religion was Zoroastrianism.
(www.zawa.asn.au) Zoroastrians were greatly persecuted by Arab invaders during the time of the second Caliph Umar.
Many converted to Islam by force and out of fear of death.
See Nedres (111:8008). 5.
To A
6. The Silk Road consisted of a series of interconnected ancient routes that crossed various territories on the Asian continent, and was essentially
It connected China to Asia Minor and the Mediterranean. It was more than 2,000 kilometers (5,000 miles) long on land and sea.
Trade on the Silk Road was an important factor in the development of the great civilizations of China, Egypt, Mesopotamia, Persia, and Rome. It laid the foundation for the modern world.
The Silk Road is the translation of the German phrase (Seidenstraße). The first person to use this term was a
There was a German geographer named F. von Richthofen in 1200.




01

The Arabian Peninsula at the time of Muhammad (showing three areas where Christians lived)


The most advanced part of the Arabian Peninsula was Yemen, where a network of dams allowed life to flourish.
Agriculture had become rich. The Kingdom of Yemen had partially adopted the Jewish faith. Their language was Arabic.
It was a southern dialect, which, although distinct, was related to the Arabic language of the rest of the peninsula. But by the late sixth century
These dams broke and the kingdom of South Arabia disintegrated. And worse, Yemen was conquered by the Persian Sassanids.
It was occupied.
Two centers of pre-Islamic Arab culture, alongside the Christian Roman Empire, Syria, and the Zoroastrian Persians,
It was located in Nahrain (Iraq). Both parts were ruled by Christian Arab kings who later
They provided the conditions for the expansion and prosperity of Muslims.
The Arabs lived in Yemen and along the coast of the Arabian Peninsula, and their occupation was agriculture and
It was fishing. On the other hand, the Arabian Peninsula was mainly inhabited by nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes.
were filled with villages, separated from the larger residential areas of Mecca and Medina. Bedouin tribes were constantly in
They were fighting each other and not marrying each other. There was no lasting peace alliance. The strongest person
Feuds were common. Attacks on other tribes were part of the unwritten law.
It was a desert.




04

The people who lived in oases became economic centers, and often when markets were established and
Nomadic people would go there for festivals. At that time, the tradition of Arabic poetry had also spread.

Arab Religion: Most Arabs at that time were animists. They had vague ideas of a supreme God who was in control of every
Something rules and dominates all (Arabic: Allah). This was necessary, but to calm the soul
Under the command of the gods on whom people believed their fate depended, in and around Mecca, for example,
Three great idols were revered: Manat, Al-'Uzzi, and Al-Ila - these three were called the daughters of Allah (Surah
19:51 A.B.) Tent dwellers and city dwellers believed that caves, trees, rivers, and rocks
They had souls and brought gifts to them. The Quraysh tribe (to which Muhammad belonged) conquered Mecca.
They built it as a center of religious activities (200-550 AD), especially the Kaaba, which was home to 120 idols and
It was considered a center of idolatry. The Kaaba (cube) was actually built in the shape of a cube and the stones
A sacred object was placed within its walls. One of the objects that was especially revered was a black stone.
Which the Arabs would visit every year and kiss out of respect.2

Judaism in that period: Since the expulsion of the Jews from Jerusalem by the Romans in 1000 AD, many Jews
They lived in the Hejaz, in the western part of the peninsula. They were skilled artisans, traders, and farmers.
They were skilled in handicrafts and were very famous for this. In Yathrib (Medina) they were like
Their ancestors lived in a separate area. The Jews considered themselves the chosen people. They worshipped God.
They believed in one God and had their own book, the Torah. Perhaps that was why Muhammad thought that
His followers also need their own book from God. Muhammad had many meetings with the Jews and
He discussed religious matters with them and learned from them many of the Talmudic texts that are still used today.
We see many of them in the Quran.

Christianity during that period: In the Roman Empire, Christians were in the majority. While in the Sasanian Empire,
Christians were important and formed an influential minority. In Arabia around 200 AD we see that
Christian churches were built in various areas near the borders, along with many small communities.
Christians in different regions, there were three main Christian regions on the outskirts of Arabia:77
1. The southwestern corner of the Arabian Peninsula, which is modern-day Yemen, especially in the city of Najran.
The factor behind its expansion was the Kingdom of Axum on the opposite coast of the Red Sea (modern-day Ethiopia and Abyssinia).
8. In the northwest, on the way to Jerusalem and Mesopotamia, some Arab tribes on the Roman borders
The East had accepted Christianity.
1. The northeastern region on the borders of Iraq was a province of the Sasanian Empire. One of
The rulers there were Nu'man ibn Mundhir 77 who converted to Christianity (208-521 AD).
In addition, numerous tribes in Arabia had converted to the Christian faith. The Arabs considered Christianity as
Foreign religions were nevertheless considered modern religions.72



7. Animists are those who believe that all elements of nature have a soul and spirit.
8. The Kaaba owes its sanctity to the Black Stone or Hajar al-Sawad. The religion of the ancient Arabs considered it obligatory to worship the gods, which
They were somehow connected to specific stones, trees, etc. (For more information, see Gibb and Kramers' book Islamology.)
(See 1951:190)
9. The Talmud is a book of rules compiled by Jewish scholars regarding Jewish law, ethics, traditions, and history.

Do it.
00. Nu'man ibn Mundhir was the king of the Ghassanids. The Ghassanids were Christian Arabs who migrated from Yemen to Harran in Syria in 850 AD.
They migrated south. The word Ghassan is derived from the Ghassanid kingdom and is also the name of a water spring in Harran.
.01 Like the Jews, Christians had a book from God as the basis of their religion – something that Muslims did not yet have.

05

Most of the Christians with whom Muhammad and the Arab people came into contact came from northern Yemen and neighboring countries.
Besides the Jewish Christians, Muhammad also confronted the Ethiopian and Syrian Christians. Many Christians
They were expelled from various places because certain new religions had established themselves in the Arabian Peninsula, which
They preserved their culture and language.

But the Christianity that Muhammad was familiar with was not a universally accepted faith, but rather a small, scattered group.
They were essentially close in the outward form of their worship.
The worship of the Virgin Mary was not unlike the idolatry of the Arabs. That is why Muhammad denounced it as idolatry.
He condemned.76

Christianity showed no unity, but instead was divided into theological disputes and power struggles between
Bishops and internal persecutions were well known. The Quran reflects a false image of a fragmented Christianity.
Muhammad had heard biblical stories, traditions, and folk customs from Jews and Christians. The way the word
The biblical references in the Quran lead us to conclude that Muhammad himself never personally read the Bible.
Unread.71
Another major influence on the Arabs came from Egyptian monasticism. Nuns often performed religious ceremonies in a
They did things that were not according to the Bible. Some participated in the pilgrimage to Mecca and followed the traditions of the
The pagan Arabs followed. Some preached openly around the Kaaba. Muhammad was very angry about this.
The monks were very interested in the Quranic statements about Christians and Jews during the first period of their ministry.74
Muhammad is relatively positive in Mecca, declaring that they should be considered primarily the People of the Book or those who possess the
The Holy Book is considered to be a book. But the statements we see from the second period in Medina are relatively negative.
It declares that they should be considered sinners who have committed polytheism (shirk) or apostasy (kufr) and have abandoned the faith.
They have returned to account and must therefore be killed. Christian churches have had an impact, especially on the borders.
Arabia had and benefited from it, but Christianity played a major role in its development and spread in the central regions.
Saudi Arabia does not have it.
71
Aam al-Fil or Year of the Elephant – the year of Muhammad's birth
Some time before the advent of Islam, King Abrahah of Abyssinia decided to strengthen the Christian faith in southern Arabia. He
He had built a church in the city of Sana'a, hoping to attract all the Arabs as pilgrims. This city in Yemen
It still stands today, but the church he built has long since disappeared. However, he decided
He was going to transform Sanaa into the commercial center of the Arabian Peninsula. As soon as his church building was finished, he ordered
He decreed that Arabs must make a pilgrimage there every year.
He was well aware of the fame of the Kaaba in Mecca and decided to move its religious and commercial status to Sana'a.
His decree was sent to the general public without a seal. But the Christian king of Yemen, Himyar, watched with sorrow as the group of pilgrims
Instead of going to Sana'a every year, they go to Mecca. 72 One day, the Christian king received news that a member of the Canaanite Arab tribe had entered
The church was destroyed and the entire interior of the church was contaminated with animal dung. When Abrahah heard that the neighboring tribes were rebelling against the laws,
He became even more furious when he heard that his ally Muhammad ibn al-Khuza'i, the king of Madar, had rebelled and killed him. In response, he
He marched on Mecca with the aim of destroying the Kaaba. A large army of infantry and cavalry was prepared.

                                                                                                                      7.2.6 Cancellation problem
Islamic jurisprudence also teaches the theory of abrogation or nullification,20 verses that have already appeared in the Quran are replaced by verses that have already appeared in the Quran.
Later they become null and void:29 We do not abrogate the verses of the Quran or the Sunnah.
                    T Bakhi
Hallelujah, God bless you
(Whatever decree We abrogate or cause to be forgotten, We bring one better than it or similar to it, except
Did you not know that Allah is over all things competent? (Surah 102:8)

This principle is often considered to be the value and superiority of the Quran over the revelations of other prophets of the Bible.
Few Muslims are aware that this principle implies contradictions in the Quran. The number of abrogated verses
It is uncertain. One Islamic scholar, Jalaluddin, mentions a figure of more than 500. Today, scholars
Various scholars have determined a maximum of 840 and a minimum of 2 abrogated verses. One of the most important abrogations is the return of

7.1.2 Hadith collection
To preserve information about Muhammad's lifestyle, after his death Muslims began to collect and record
The words and events of his life that were not in the Quran were revealed. These stories and events, which are mainly oral, are
English is known as news and can be divided into two subgroups, Hadith (16 sayings, stories) and
Sunnah (11 customs and traditions) Today they are available in collected text form.
Sunnah includes three categories:
1. The deeds of Muhammad or the omitted ones.
8. Actions that some of the Muslim companions of Muhammad committed in his presence and are considered to be approved.
It was him.
1. Acts performed in the presence of Muhammad by a third party that he did not approve or prohibit.

Hadith and Sunnah include the actions and words narrated from Muhammad and his contemporaries and are considered authentic and influential.
But many rituals are created and elaborated out of expediency, not to promote a particular theory or opinion.
Someone provides for someone. Muslim scholars have tried to separate false traditions from authentic traditions.
Muslims soon realized that many traditions were not from Muhammad or his companions and therefore
They are not acceptable. About 800 years after Muhammad, some scholars tried to collect them.
And they tried to determine which was authentic and which was fake.

The proof of the acceptability and validity of the traditions were:
1. The Sunnah must be attributed with complete reliability to its chain of narrators and be based on the credibility of the narrator (Isnaad).
8. Each narrator in the chain must be accepted as a credible person with knowledge of the matter to be trusted.
1. The contents of the text must be tested in different places and not contradict other traditions.

One of the most famous narrators of Sunni hadith is Sahih Bukhari (d. 200 CE) and the other is Muslim.
(d. 205 CE). Bukhari collected about 200,000 hadiths of Muhammad's words and deeds.

The importance of these hadiths has increased over time and for many Muslims
It has even gained an importance equivalent to the importance of the Quran and its role as a guide.

It enlightens Islamic societies and provides a comprehensive set of rules for resolving religious questions.

A new dimension emerged in the field of religious duties. Judicial issues in the Quran were sufficient to formulate a religious system.
To solve these problems, Muslims eventually accepted the theory that Muhammad's entire way of life was
And his actions (Sunnah) were determined by the inspiration of Allah. Therefore, those Sunnahs (Hadith) that directly refer to
It was attributed to Muhammad himself and was accepted as obligatory. For this reason, the Sunnah is important to many Muslims.
As a revelation from Allah, it even gained a value equal to the Quran. If Muhammad was truly a role model for Islamic societies,
It is, legally, only his words and actions are obligatory for all Muslims. The Sunnah at that time, according to the
It became the second authoritative source for Islamic law.
During the 19th century and possibly the 1980s, due to the new challenges faced by the Muslims of the world,
The precedent of Islamic law changed. The West had become a global power and a large part of the world, including
had colonized Muslim territory. Many societies had transformed from agriculture to industry.

Equality changed. Islamic societies, especially the Ottoman Empire, were declining. The cries of

7.6.2 Four authoritative references
When a problem arises for which the Quran and Hadith do not provide a solution, how should Muslims decide?
The way out of this problem is the principle of ijtihad (the use of human reasoning). The decision rule
The practice or code of professional conduct based on the sources of Sharia (Quran and Sunnah). As a result of analogy and consensus, the third
And the fourth way is to achieve justice. In the three centuries after the death of Muhammad,

1. The Quran: This is the first source and most important basis for Islamic law.
8. Sunnah: The habits and sayings of the Prophet, which are available in collections of hadith.
1. Analogy: A problem recently solved by drawing conclusions from comparisons using the laws of logical inference and
Following the known cases is solved.
4. Ijma: If there are still unresolved issues that the Quran, Hadith, and analogy cannot resolve,
The consensus of the Islamic community is binding. In practice, this (consensus) is limited to the agreement of 27 Muslim theologians.
It is.

However, there are different opinions among theologians regarding interpretation and application.

Islamic habits and their inherent habits act vigilantly. The differences between them are in
Numerous cases have led to outbreaks of prejudice and conflict.

Customary laws are not easily discovered and collected. It is written in indigenous sources that these laws,
The rulings of the local leaders are. These books cannot be judged according to Western thinking.
They are based on the thoughts of the jurists themselves, and do not always correspond to conventional rulings. Often
Customary laws have been added to Sunni Islamic law. For example, marriage may be based on customary laws.
Islamic rituals are performed, but the celebration they hold is in accordance with customary laws. From an Islamic point of view,
The validity of marriage is derived from Islamic rulings. In practice, the elimination of this celebration invalidates the marriage.

It is "devotion". God expects man to remember him as a great and knowledgeable person,
And obey him and submit to him. Islam in its true sense means surrender, which includes an expectation
that, through this submission, man attains peace. The word Muslim (meaning one who has devoted his whole life to
(dedicated to Allah with devotion) also comes from the same root (sin al-mim). A religious Muslim
He does not follow, but his entire life path is provided by Allah, who governs all aspects of life.
It is his individual and social.

In Islamic theory, these classifications are very different; the doctrine
The articles of faith, which include six topics, and Islamic practice (religion), which includes five duties.

7.1.4 Apostasy or turning away from Islam.
The act of turning away from religion or apostasy practically means going back. Leaving Islam for another religion.
or a godless way of life. During the lifetime of Muhammad and the caliphs, a number of Muslims apostatized from the faith, either
They were killed immediately or given a few days to return to Islam. Both the Quran and Hadith
They teach the death penalty for apostasy. The Hadith is even clearer on this matter than the Quran.
According to Sunni Sharia law, a man (apostate) is given three days to make a decision; if he does not return
She must be killed. An apostate woman is not sentenced to death, but she can be kept in prison until she returns.
If a minor boy apostatizes, he should not be killed. But he should remain in prison until he reaches the age of responsibility.
If he continues to be unfaithful, he must be killed after that.
There are many guidelines regarding this issue.180 However, Muslim scholars and schools of thought in some
The details are disputed. This law is a cruel apostasy and against human rights. To the extent that
It is usually not implemented directly by Islamic governments, but rather by family members or leaders.
Religion is practiced.

Everyone has the right to freedom of thought, opinion and religion. This right includes freedom to change religion or belief.
This freedom is also available to anyone, either alone or in association with others, openly or in private, to proclaim their religion or belief.
It also includes belief, teaching, worship, and the performance of rituals.

7.4.7 Origin of Shia and Sunni
The word Sunni is derived from the word Sunnah, which means the beliefs and customs of the Prophet Muhammad. About 90% of Muslims
Of all Muslims, 10% are Sunni. The remaining 10% are distributed among Shiites and other groups, which are divided due to differences.
The differences between the two major Islamic groups were originally only related to family.721
There is: Early Muslims disputed over the succession of the Prophet Muhammad, who had no guidance regarding
He had not left behind a successor. While the Sunnis had taken over the leadership (political and therefore religious)
accepted Islam, the Shiites rejected the legitimacy of the first three caliphs and rebelled against them. The Shiites
They only accepted Ali, the Prophet's son-in-law, who was physically descended from Muhammad and his legal heir.
It was for the political and religious leadership of Muslims.

The Shiites had endless disputes that led to divisions, for example over the question of whether the Quran was created or not.
They have existed since time immemorial, and they defended the predestination of Allah against human responsibility. 726 The Shiites also believe this.

Sufism: Mysticism in Islam 7.4.1
In general, Islam is a Sharia-based religion that can cause reactions in those who
They seek freedom from impersonal legalism. Sufism has developed over the centuries as a reaction to
Shariati-based Islam has developed. It includes a variety of beliefs and practices. The way
(Sufi orders) can come from Shia Islam, Sunni Islam, other Islamic sects, or a combination of multiple traditions.
Sufism emerged in the Middle East in the eighth century. But today its adherents are found all over the world.
They are spread all over the world. Their traditional view is that the world originated from Suf, an Arabic word meaning
It is wool, and refers to the woolen robes worn by early ascetic Muslims. But all
Sufis do not wear robes or woolen garments. Another lexical theory holds that the root word Sufi is
The Arabic word is Safa, which means purity and cleanliness.

This theory emphasizes the purity of the heart and soul. Around 1100 CE, al-Ghazali attempted to emphasize the interpretation of
The spiritual dimension of human commitments prevents Islamic theology and jurisprudence from becoming fossilized. But Sufism has even older roots.
There was a key female Sufi named Rabia Basri, who practiced pure love for Allah and strict principles of asceticism.
Islamic Sufis borrowed many concepts from other religions, especially Hindus, Buddhists, and
And Christians have borrowed it, which has shaped their view of the concept of Allah. They have taught their followers
Certain methods of meditation and self-control by repeating the name of Allah or other religious words 711 times a thousand times
The goal of these exercises is to become completely one with Allah. Sufis also call Muhammad a Sufi.
Many Muslims practice Sufism and at the same time consider themselves Sunni.
They know. Today, many Sufi leaders are revered as saints, and their tombs are considered sacred.
Shrines are visited by many. Muslims expect to receive blessings from visiting such places.
They have a special meaning. There is no doubt that secrets and mysteries play an important role in Sufism.

Sufis gather in small personal groups and believe that the interaction between master and disciple is for
Student growth is essential. They make extensive use of parables, similes, and metaphors. They believe in concepts
which can only be achieved through a period of truth-seeking and individual awareness of a secret perception. Although
Philosophy varies between different Sufi orders, Sufism as a whole is about direct personal experiences.
It is and therefore can be compared to different forms of Sufism such as Zen, Buddhism, mysticism, etc.
The following parable is attributed to an unknown Sufi scholar, which illustrates this line of thought:
There are three ways to know something. Take a flame for example. A person might only know it in
Someone may have heard about the flame. Someone else may have seen the flame with their own eyes. And finally, someone else
It can touch the flame and burn. We Sufis seek to be burned by Allah.

7.1.6 Timing of Islamic holidays
The Islamic year is based on the phases of the moon (Ahlul-Qamar). Therefore, it is eleven days shorter than the
It is solar. Therefore, the months and holidays occur ten or eleven days earlier each year. An Islamic century
It is three years shorter than the 711th century AD. The first day of the Islamic calendar is 288 AD, which is
Muhammad migrated from Mecca to Medina. The lunar month has 154 days and is divided into 18 months, each of which is between
It is 89 or 10 days.

I wished I could find these tourists and ask these simple questions that

Dealing with Muslim children is challenging and requires a lot of self-sacrifice.
There is patience, love and empathy from Christians. Children come from different cultural and family backgrounds.
They come. Some have trouble with different languages and cultures. Many children live a double life.
They have: The modern and intellectual culture of society is often compared to the strict Islamic culture that dominates the home.
In many children, this dual lifestyle leads to hostility. More problems
It arises when Muslim students in the modern education system need to work independently to
Critical questions. Muslims in general are not allowed and were not raised to ask critical questions.
Do it.

Most children go to Islamic centers where Quran courses are held. They read the texts.
They learn Arabic, but usually do not understand what they read. 214 They are given an introduction to ethics and
Islamic thought is taught, which often prepares children to oppose Christian teachings.

So children have little information about Christianity, the Bible, and Jesus; that little information.
Whatever they acquire is largely distorted and wrong. They usually do not have a complete view of Christianity.
Children in different countries develop differently, influenced by the habits of their parents and society.
Christians working with Muslim children and youth are strongly advised to:
Obtain sufficient information through special books and seminars.

Prayer and practical help.

Show love and invite them to your home if possible.

Regular parent-teacher meetings at home to build trust in both children and parents.
Parents are essential.

To be explained.

They are for advertising.

You go to the nearest bookstore and buy a book that
It's titled How to Become a Good Swimmer, and it's titled

Rotten eggs
Muslims think they can atone for their bad deeds with good deeds without the sacrifice of Jesus.
give good deeds, and then Allah will allow them to enter Paradise. Muslims must understand that Allah
They take sin seriously and should know that sins are forgiven only according to God's plan. An example:
A housewife prepared a dough using a few eggs. She mistakenly put a rotten egg in it.

Won't rotten chicken make a difference? No – everyone knows that one rotten egg will ruin the whole dough.

Jumping off a cliff

But the poor man doesn't want to accept the car as a gift, he wants to pay at least a little for it.
Wouldn't the host be upset about this? He wanted to give the car as a gift, just as God had promised.
He wants to give eternal life as a gift.

Four reports

Four friends are looking at an accident scene. The police arrive and ask

Which one will be valid? The police will definitely think there is something wrong.
There are four people in these writings who colluded and prepared the same report.

The animal's blood flows into a prepared pit. After the animal is skinned, it is divided into three parts.
One part is usually given to relatives, the second part to the poor, and the third part for oneself.
The family remains. The sacrifice is performed only to remember the sacrifice of Abraham. The Quran has no spiritual meaning.

A. Will find new perspectives to bring Muslims to Christ.
B. Church members will be encouraged to pray for new activities and Muslim conversions.
C. Christians will be educated and their fear of approaching Muslims will disappear.
D. Christians will become more effective in witnessing to Muslims and the door to new friendships will open.
E. Biblical information will be given greater attention and Christians will learn how to
Share their faith with them in a way that Muslims can understand.
And. Basic knowledge of Islam will enable Christians to better communicate with Muslims.
G. The issue of Muslim conversion will be carried out under the supervision of trained Christians.

1.1.1Arab Spring
A series of demonstrations and protests took place in North Africa and the Middle East in late 2010, leading to
The Arab Spring became known. The protests began on December 12th following the self-immolation of Mohamed Bouaziz in protest against
Police behavior and social corruption that prevailed in society began in Tunisia. With the victory of the protests in Tunisia, a wave
From the sparks of the Tunisian burning man to Algeria, Libya, Egypt, Jordan, Syria, Yemen and other countries in the region
The largest and most organized demonstration on the day known as the Day of Rage, after
Friday prayers were held. Many protesters faced violent reactions from governments, and violence often erupted.
Anti-government protests were suppressed by militias. In many cases, these attacks were violent.
The protesters were responding.

By September 2018, governments had been overthrown in four countries. Tunisian President Zine El Abidine Ben Ali
On January 14, 2011, he fled to Saudi Arabia following revolutionary protests. Egyptian President Hosni Mubarak
Mubarak was removed from office on February 11, 2018, following 12 days of violent protests and replaced by a third president.
His reign was over. In Libya, Muammar Gaddafi was overthrown on August 1, 2011, and in the city of Tripoli on October 1, 2011,
His hometown was killed by revolutionaries after the National Transitional Council took control. Chairman
Yemeni President Ali Abdullah Saleh accepts Gulf Council's power transfer agreement in exchange for letter of truce
177 and presidential elections were held, with Abd Rabbuh Mansur al-Hadi officially elected in 1980.
He took his place in February 2018.

During the local unrest, 8018 several leaders were removed from power at the end of the interim presidency.
Their republics declared their readiness. Sudanese President Omar al-Bashir announced that in 2015 he would seek
There is no re-election, and neither is Iraqi President Nouri al-Maliki, whose term ends in 2014.
He has announced that he will not seek re-election, although there are many protests calling for his immediate removal.
Protests in Jordan have also caused King Abdullah to change his cabinet four times in a row.
Public unrest in Kuwait also led to the resignation of Prime Minister Nasser Mohammed Al-Ahmad Al-Sabah.

Many Arabs aspire to a better life and democratic freedom like in Western countries. But is it possible?
Have these protests really fulfilled their wish? Do the general public enjoy freedom of religion?
Including religious minorities such as Christians? Are human rights respected and are both men and women entitled to the same rights?
Do they benefit equally? Has this demonstration, which cost the lives of thousands of people, led to change and growth?
Has the economy recovered? Has violence and corruption really stopped?

In early 2011, these popular uprisings seemed to have led to the rise of conservative Islam and
The Syrian war in 2011 was just one of these.
It is clear that the situation of Christians is more ambiguous than ever, and even if they are not killed, because
Prejudices will have a hard life. In this way, it can be said that this series of events not only led to the Arab Spring,
It will not follow, but will follow the Arab winter.

However, there is another aspect. In Egypt, for example, like many of its neighbors, there are ongoing movements.

1.2.1 Adaptation to the environment or adaptation
The word adaptation was introduced as a specific evangelical term in the ministry in 1908, when
The Theological Cultural Fund, under the auspices of the World Council of Churches, has released its report on its service in the field.
published, in which the churches were strongly urged to implement fundamental reforms in theological teachings.
This includes feminist theology, liberation theology, and theology known as
It was black theology. Since then, there have been intense discussions and debates from various sects around this
The topic has been ongoing. Adaptation, in short, includes all the things that mean localization.
It involves the construction and development of national theology and the adaptation of Christianity to specific groups of people.

Adaptation is indeed a new ministry term, but its roots lie in the life and work of the early churches.
Paul, in particular, was a very ground-breaking preacher who presented a context-dependent theology and approach.

6.6 Arabic/Islamic Glossary
The list below contains concepts taken from both Arab and Islamic tradition that
It provides more vocabulary from the Arabic language. Separating the concepts of Islam from the concepts specific to Arab culture or
The Arabic language itself can be difficult. Many Arabic concepts have both Islamic and secular meanings.
One example of this is da'wah. The complexity of the Arabic language is that a single word can be
The word Islam itself is a good example. Readers should also note that
Arabic is written with its own alphabet, with letters, symbols, and conventional writing that has no equivalent in
It does not have the Latin alphabet (see Arabic letters). This is a letter-by-letter list of Arabic words and vocabulary.
It is written. So Muslims may translate certain words differently, for example, religion versus deen.
The spelling and orthography of Arabic words can vary in English texts. In this book, the method
A transcription has been adopted that allows the reader to pronounce the word more or less correctly. Wherever
Where necessary, notes on how to pronounce words are given in parentheses.
